# I-0113 « Les écarts ouverts » - result

## Conclusion

**Complete (2026-11-12, closed at ROADMAP workstream 3's end; budget 131 of 140).** Forty-one tranches merged from 2026-10-30 to
2026-11-12 (the last `e305469c`). Of the 27 numbered gaps (gap 10 never existed: no record defines it), Thomas validated five in
play, nine are fixed and wait for his eye, two were fixed under the raster lighting that no longer exists, three are not a native
defect or were not reproduced, three are closed by his decisions, one is dropped and three stay open and go to ROADMAP workstream
5 (gaps 11 and 26 have two parts each and are counted once, 11 with the decisions and 26 with the fixes). The table below is the
final state; the text after it is the history, tranche by tranche, as it was written (an earlier sentence that the table
contradicts is superseded by it). Since the closure: gap 23 fixed under the TAA by I-0140 tranche 12 and validated by Thomas in
play (2026-11-17 05:47), its row updated; gap 16 at 1920x1080 of the original's order on the effects' clock and the Quit Game
portrait's background and hair as the original's for Alpha (I-0140 tranche 26), their rows updated.

  mist natively by gap 24's cut); the menus' portrait cases (gap 3's Ringed City quit, the Quit
  Game portrait's hair, face and background, « Tranche 31 »); the TAA's reactive weighting where effects cover most of the screen
  (0.38 to 0.52 of Archdragon Peak's frame at rest, its look not judged, its threshold against FSR 2's unverified; « Tranche 41 »).
- To workstream 4 (robustness): the exit fault at `DarkSoulsIII.exe+0xF14C52`, not attributed (« Tranche 38 »).
- To workstream 15 (the second optimisation review, whose ROADMAP text now names them): the 3x3 reactive form's GPU cost and the
  reactive weighting's copy, unmeasured (« Tranche 39 », « Tranche 41 »); the walls' follow effect asking E6DCD0 again about once a
  frame (« Tranche 40 »).
- To workstream 16 (the reviews' « recorded, left » points): « Uncertainty » below, its rows for tranches 12 to 17.

The history, tranche by tranche:

Opened 2026-10-30. Gap 1, Anor Londo's grey walls: not found in three runs; found in Thomas's NVIDIA clip of 26 October 05:20
(`Videos/NVIDIA/Dark Souls III/Dark Souls III 2026.10.26 - 05.20.42.04.mp4`, 0:36 and 0:48: in the hall of the great staircase behind
the side entrance, past the rubble, the far walls flat grey; `evidence/I-0113/anor-londo/`), reproduced at a teleport into that hall (`i113-al1`): the original's brick walls drawn flat grey natively. Fixed in tranche 6 (merged `42360bd9`, start check `start-42360bd9` passed): the wall is
`m37_00_00_00_010300` mesh 0 (`m37_00_bldgA_wall_00`, `M[ARSN]_p`, parallax occlusion mapping, `GXFlver_ColDifSpcPomIbl`); its b2
`cbMatDynParam` +0x40..+0x5F (the POM height scale and sample counts) was zero natively, because the native re-reading of the GX items
141070 parses (141070 does not run natively) skipped GX02 and GX12; it reads them now and copies 22E930's 32-byte block, and the
wall's b2 +0x40..+0x5F equals the original's (0.01, 0, 8, 64, 0.58), its bricks as the original's (FLIP 0.069 against 0.171 before).
Every material with a GX02 or GX12 item took the same zeros before (K-0487; 140 materials carry GX02 and 44 GX12, `gx-census.tsv`, K-0488). The CONTRACT's hypotheses (a white fallback, a wrong slot or programme, an `_l` holder) are refuted for this wall: its textures
and programme are the original's, its POM constants were zero.
Tranche 7 (the census of that class, [ZERO-CONSTANTS-CENSUS](ZERO-CONSTANTS-CENSUS.md), K-0488): every FLVER's GX items read offline and every programme's RDEF; the native now reads every item a material carries whose word a programme reads (GX36 and GX37, the FlowDisplacement materials of four characters; GX39, FC_SoftVolume of two m50 effects; GX40 for completeness); live at three witness zones, on the 748 draws matched to the original's by model, PS and index count, no PS b2 or b3 word is zero natively where the original fills it (364 draws unmatched); FC_SSS_ID differs on the player's skin; b4 (gap 7) not yet comparable. Merged `6505dbb7` 2026-10-31 (with R1's quit fix), the launcher rebuilt, start check `start-6505dbb7` passed (344 world frames, a clean exit, restored).
Gap 2, the Handmaid's chair: fixed (tranche 1, merged `98a86ebc`, start check `start-98a86ebc` passed). Its light and fog key {m40, -1} (an area, no gparam id) took default.gparam's
{1, 0} set natively (colours (1, 1, 1, 2.5) and two more lights) where the original resolves it to a registered node holding the
default values (colours (1, 1, 1, 1)); such a key now takes the engine lookup, whose built-in record natively carries the same
values (but for its words f[60..63]), and the chair is drawn dark as the original's. Thomas validated it in play (2026-10-31 07:16, « la chaise de la servante: validé »). Gap 3, the Load Game portrait: not reproduced in 20 openings; a per-view state witness stays in the module. Gap 4, the
banding in dark areas with red and blue patches: found and fixed in tranche 2 (merged `e38d260e`, start check `start-e38d260e` passed). Since the I-0109 ray-tracing
self-test runs at start (30 October), the pipelines a start loaded from the driver's pipeline library sampled through their
static samplers as point samplers, so the grading read its 16-cube LUT at the nearest cell and the glow came out in blocks
(K-0486; with the library loaded and the self-test off, smooth). The native loads no library any more and compiles its
precompile list at every start (D-0241). Gap 7, the Undead Settlement wheels lighter natively: still so (1.08 of the original in tranche 5's view); the model is `o001279`,
its texture light as natively drawn, its light set its neighbours' in both renderers (envData eliminated, tranche 5); cause open. Gap 5, the Estus Flask missing from the hand while drinking (Thomas, 2026-10-28): the Ashen
Estus Flask was drawn (its flask model particle `s07002` in the effect pass 0x29), the orange Estus Flask was not (the same model
in the effect pass 0x21, which the native counted and did not draw); tranche 3 (merged `7b6068e3`, start check `start-98b1f907` passed) draws the model particles of pass 0x21 in
the row 0x21, and the orange flask is in the hand, opaque, as in the original; the Ashen flask is drawn see-through (additive, the
0x29 effect context's blend) where the original's is opaque: tranche 5 keys the native's 0x29 forward rows as the original does (the keys equal) and gives a particle the state of the row before it, so the flask's draws took the state of the row before them; tranche 9 (gap 17) found that the original's mesh loop rebinds both states for every model particle (the material's blend-mode parameter and Begin's depth: alpha and written through a whole drink), and replaced that inheritance. Tranche 4 (merged `cbfbf1a6`, start check `start-cbfbf1a6` passed): witnesses only, which establish that the original's Ashen flask inherits the material binds of a type-3 model row before it in its chunk of the scene view's sub 0x29, and that the original draws the wheels with the native's PS and b3 (gaps 5 and 7 below). Gap 6, the Dancer of the Boreal Valley hall (High Wall of Lothric) darker natively (2026-10-19): now as the
original's (regions 0.99-1.04, FLIP 0.030).

The gaps Thomas saw in play on 2026-10-31 (07:18-07:47, `main`'s launcher; his captures in `Pictures/Screenshots`):

- Gap 8: at the Firelink Shrine bonfire the character takes a strong orange light with vertical streaks on the skin and trousers, with and without DLSS (07:18, 07:27). The original does not show it (Thomas, 07:20; checked in the original at the bonfire, 08:46: no streaks, a smooth warm light on the back; pair `evidence/I-0113/gaps-0731/bonfire-original-084644.png` and `bonfire-native-071810.png`). Thomas (08:19-08:20): right with the popin launcher on a first load, wrong after a return to the title and a reload. **Fixed in tranche 8 (the cause VERIFIED statically, the fix seen):** the original's local face pass 10AFE0 records per container item the commands 1400BEEB0 and 1400BEEC0, which set the draw context's +0x718 = (item +0x1840 != 0) and +0x724 = item +0x1844, and 14010F710 (+0x714 = 0); 5A120's preparation 2 then sets the draw state +0x180 bit 7 from +0x718 (64610's cull row 1 {0,2,1}: the face set's cull class flipped) and, for the pass ids 9..0x38, the rasterizer key's mode 3 (pass +0x760 bits 5-6) with the key byte +0x761 = +0x724; 1401012E0 / 140101420 build that table: mode 3 is DepthBias = the key byte, clamp 0, slope-scaled bias 3.0, front counter-clockwise, depth clip (the GX description mapped by 14192F860; mode 1, the cascades', slope 3.0; mode 2 the velocity's words +0x9B8..+0x9C0). The item's +0x1840/+0x1844 are B29D0's last two arguments, the frame item's +0x14/+0x18 (2136B0 / 212810: the light's +0xB2 bool and +0xB4 int), which the BTL loader 140018490 reads from each light's bytes +0xA3 and +0xA2 (BTL versions 4 and later): in the 29 BTL files of `gamedata-i0110` every point and spot light of versions 5 and 6 carries the bias 2 and, but for 146, the flip 1 (`Get-I113BtlBias.py`, `evidence/I-0113/gap8/btl-bias.txt`; version 2 files carry neither, the lights keep their defaults). The native drew the local casters with the cascade rows' rasterizer (cull none, no constant bias, slope 0.005): the faces shadowed themselves (the « state not reset between loads » lead is refuted: the original's reload differs from its first load the same way, below). Now every caster row of a frame gets, once an item has given a bias (kept across frames), two more pipelines with the mode-3 state (the cull class as is and flipped), and each local face binds the one its item's +0x1840 asks (`native_local_shadow`'s `local_raster`: [bias known, items not flipped, bias, items with another bias]; the runs above logged the first version's `raster`: [set, flip, bias, mixed]). Runs `i113-g8-main2` (main's module), `i113-g8-fix2` (2026-10-31 11:36-11:42) and `i113-g8-orig` (the original, 12:18-12:21): Eta's Firelink save, half a camera turn so the bonfire lights the back, Quit Game and Continue (`Run-I113Bonfire.ps1`): main, no bonfire light on the first load and dark streaks after the reload (Thomas's defect reproduced); the fix and the original, warm on the first load and darker on the left leg after the reload (`evidence/I-0113/gap8/bonfire-main-fix-orig-crops.png`: main, fix, original, each first load then reload). The review's per-face flip and kept bias (below) were built after these runs.
- Gap 9: some filaments of the lightning spells are black with a gold rim beside bright ones: Lightning Spear (07:21, 07:26), Lightning Stake (07:31) and Great Lightning Spear, most of them (07:32): the family's common filament, the shape of the trails (606 or 10012, I-0105). Not from the census: the bolt model `s03000` (`S[A]_add_Soft`) carries GX39 = 0, the original's FC_SoftVolume as the native's. The cause is the blend and depth: every original draw of the crystals (861) and of the flask through a whole drink (110 sampled over 4.9 s) blends SRC_ALPHA / INV_SRC_ALPHA and writes depth LESS_EQUAL, set inside the mesh loop (OBSERVED), where the native drew them with the effect context's state at 636F0's entry, additive 0x20 and depth unwritten 0x1E8 (I-0105, and tranche 5's row for the flask). The study thread found the setter and the stack confirms it (`15220` ← `140200840`, slot 1 of `GXFlverMaterialParamBlendMode` ← `14002E5C0` ← Begin `140140680`; depth `19C590`): the index is the table `14473E5C0` [the group's `+0x85`, the FFX command's `+0x30` byte][the material's blend mode], with the draw arguments' `+0x200` modifier bits, bank entry 8 + 8 × index (VERIFIED). Natively `NativeEffectModelBlend` applies that rule to every E03 particle (`8c88cf4d`); its depth is Begin's (140140680): for a material class without the bits 0x10D, written (0x1E0) when the arguments' `+0x188` is nonzero and depth write allowed, which 29ADF0 sets to `-(command[0] != 0)` after 59470 (the final review, checked in the assembly), else 0x1E8; for the other classes the forward pair `[[GXRS+0x4DE8]+0x860]` (the bank's 0x1E0 with a stencil) when `+0x188` is nonzero and the MTD's `+0x111` clear, else `+0x868` (0x1E8): the crystals and the flask (class 1) take `+0x860`, written (the study thread's read, STUDY-TRANSLUCENT-CHAMELEON §6). `i113-t9-crystal-fixed`: blend 0x10, depth 0x1E0 natively, the crystals violet, faceted and opaque as the original's, their hue still different (Thomas, 21:37; the forward rows' IBL is still the black cube of policy 1114: the next tranche) ([sheet](../../evidence/I-0113/gap17/crystal-before-fixed-orig-2-1600.png): native before, fixed, original); `i113-t9-flask-fixed`: the Ashen flask at the mouth as the original's ([sheet](../../evidence/I-0113/gap17/flask-hand-fixed-top-original-bottom.png)). Tranche 5's row inheritance (`ResolveNativeEffectRowStates`) read the state at 636F0's entry, which the mesh loop replaces: removed, with `no-effect-row-states.flag` (the origins it read stay as `native_effect_model_first`'s witness). The rule and its study: [STUDY-TRANSLUCENT-CHAMELEON](STUDY-TRANSLUCENT-CHAMELEON.md) §5.

Tranche 13 (branch `claude/charming-antonelli-e914e7`, 2026-11-02 morning, the ASUS, Thomas asleep): **the model particles' colour fixed.** The cause (STATIC, headless Ghidra on the mirror, the step review re-read A7E40's case 0x26 and 61A20's assembly): 27E5E0, the E03 model set's factory, stores 61A20(model) at set+0x129, and 61A20 answers 1 when a material of the model's table (+0x1F0, vector +0x1F8..+0x200) carries bit 20 (0x100000) of its word +0x24; 27EA70 then multiplies the colour by the draw context's +0xAA8 (0.652). Bit 20 is `GXFT_InvalidLighting` (builtin kind 38; 13 MTDs, all True: `s[a]*`, `m[a]*`, the families GXFlver_Col, GXFlver_ColDif, GXFlver_ColZFeather; MTD-FLAG-BITS-INVENTORY-SHEET 2.1), which A7E40 sets in gxMtd+0x88 and 140F90 copies to material+0x24; natively neither runs and the instance word took three bits by hand (0x2000, 0x800, 0x2), not this one. Now the material instance's construction publishes it (`18_resource_admission.inc`, A7E40's low bit of the first value); no native reader of the word or of the aggregate model+0x224 tests bit 20 (the step review's census). - **Gap 16, not reproduced; the case of a burst on the ground close by with the bright sky behind not tested.** Thomas's capture (`Pictures/Screenshots/Capture d'écran 2026-10-31 080219.png`) shows the ring only where the burst covers the bright fog and sky behind a dead tree a few metres away; the three earlier tries cast against dark stone. `i113-t18-orb-native` (ffx-record), `-original` (ffx-draw-observe), `-nocopy` (no-ffx-effect-copy) at Archdragon Peak, the camera turned half a turn to the cliff, four Fire Orbs each (11:18-11:35), then `-near-native` and `-near-original` with a step forward first (11:59-12:08): every orb burst in the sky over the cliff, none on the ground; the bursts alike in the five runs, no magenta or cyan (`burst-c1-native-original-nocopy.png`, `burst-c2-…`, `near-c1-…` to `near-c4-native-original.png`, OBSERVED at 10 frames/s). The Normal 608's constants (`ffx_distortion_witness`, 96 rows with a nonzero blend in each mode) are equal in both: blend 2, `g_ps_ColorBlendType` 0 (the product branch), colour scale (1, 1, 1, 1) on 82 rows and (1.5, 1.5, 1.5, 1) on 14, `aa8` 0.625, the same `g_color` ramp (OBSERVED). So the 608 itself, given the same copy, should compute the original's colour (INFERRED: the native draws the original's programme with these constants); only a non-finite value in the scene copy would kill a channel (STUDY-GAPS-16-13 §3, INFERRED), and none showed. The PIX run (`-pix`, four captures with `pix-f8.txt` « 70 12 ») caught the frames before the cast, not the burst (captures deleted, disposable); it closed on the known fault of WinPixGpuCapturer.dll under the module's static destruction at the DLL's detach (`crashes/startup-crash1.txt`, as tranche 12's M1), not a play fault. Thomas's capture predates I-0116 (DLSS) and its scene looks brighter than today's native picture (by eye, not measured); INSUFFICIENT INFORMATION (this tranche: three native conditions, against the bright sky, with the effect copy off, after a step forward). A reliable test of the remaining case needs an aim at the ground close by (a teleport facing a near rock with the sky above, or a lock-on), not done.
- **Pass 0x23 (the water interaction map t57): nothing in the shipped effects draws into it.** STUDY-WATER-INTERACTION §4.3 routes an FFX entity to pass 0x23 when its root action 10500 has field 6 (+0x18) nonzero (or, with field 6 at 0, a property slot of the action). A scan of the 5,187 extracted FXRs of every bundle (`Get-I113Fxr10500.ps1`, a Sonnet subagent's script, the pilot spot-checked its CSV and one dump, then re-ran it with the property counts after the final review's M2): 5,181 carry a root 10500, always with nine fields; field 6 and field 8 (+0x20, the reflection-view flag) are 0 in all, and every one has no property (props1 = props2 = 0, fields2 = 0) (OBSERVED, `evidence/I-0113/t18/fxr10500-all.csv` and `fxr10500-output.txt`; that the dump's field order is the record's offset order is INFERRED from the schema). So t57 stays neutral in the shipped game, and the native's grey default (SYSTEX_GRAY) is exact; the clear each frame is moot. Closed.
- **Gap 21 (the hair in a fast travel), reproduced; a candidate cause located (INFERRED), not fixed.** Thomas's clips (2026-11-02 08:54 native, 08:56 original) show a warp from the Archdragon Peak bonfire in daylight. `Run-I113Warp.ps1` (Alpha's all-pyromancies copy: Firelink, travel to Archdragon Peak, rest there again after a 60 ms step, since the rest prompt stays down after a warp until an input, `i113-t18-warp-*` and `-warp2-*` failed so; then the travel back filmed, `travel2.mp4`), `i113-t18-warp3-native` and `-original` (12:51-13:01): the body fades alike; the hair does not (`warp3-head3x-native-top-original-bottom.png`, 3x at 7.5 frames/s): the original's hair breaks into sparse pixels, a dither dissolve, from the fade's first steps; the native's stays a solid dark cap, then fades as one smooth translucent cap (OBSERVED). Natively (`i113-t18-warp4-native`, build-u2 with the named draw witness extended: `native_named_draw_sources` now also each twentieth of b4's ditherFadeAlpha, with the dither words, mulCol.w, FC_AlphaRef, the blend and the depth write): during the fade the hair's meshes 6 and 10 leave 0x1F for 0x29 (category 0x29), a DptA pre-pass (recipe 21) then `SAT_HairIbl2_Fwd_[A]` (recipe 7) blended SRC_ALPHA / INV_SRC_ALPHA with depth written, ditherFadeAlpha = mulCol.w = the opacity (1 down to 0.17), FC_isDitherAlpha 1, **FC_DitherEnable 0** (OBSERVED): the programme's dither discard (`_[A]`'s last block: FC_DitherEnable > 0, FC_isDitherAlpha, (dither - 0.5) × 0.999 + ditherFadeAlpha < 0.5) never runs, hence the smooth fade. FC_DitherEnable is PS b7 +0x20 = 1 when the draw state's +0x200 bit 20 (1A86C0:203), which 5A120 sets from Q+0x1A9 (5A120:157-165), which 5C0E0 sets (5C0E0:101-115) when the source group's +0x88 is set with its opacity under the category scalar, or, for a single row of preparation 2, its +0x89 (VERIFIED in the decompiles). The original's fade inputs at 5C0E0's call (new `reference_row_fade_inputs`, light-observe, `i113-t18-warp6-original`, 14:42-14:47): for HR_A_0101 during the fade +0x84..+0x89 = (0xFF, 1, 0, 0, **0, 1**), the opacity 0.99 down to 0.26 (the witness's 400 rows ran out there, the face parts taking 333 of them; now 800 rows, the hair only), and three rows a step at engine pass 0x29: preparation 1 (the pre-pass), preparation 0 (the colour) and **preparation 2, recorded alone** (OBSERVED): that row reads +0x89 = 1 and so takes Q+0x1A9 = 1, the dither; the two others read +0x88 = 0, as the native's. So by the same static chain the original's colour row is not dithered by FC_DitherEnable either: the dissolve has to come through the preparation-2 row, which is not shown yet (INFERRED). Where the 0x29 preparation-2 row is recorded is not found: none of 57A20's 5C0E0 / 5C060 records is (0x29, 2) (STATIC, its disassembly read: its 0x29 records take preparation 0 or 1; corrected in tranche 19: the records whose pass is the model's category, in views 1..0x38, are); the 5C0E0 xrefs list DATA references too (INFERRED: another emitter, perhaps CFDE70's own path for a part). The original's named oracle (decal-observe, « !hr_a_0101|fg_a_0100 » during the travel, `-SecondTravelNamed` and `-SecondTravelNamedAfter`, `i113-t18-warp4-original`, `-warp5-original`) never covered the fade (it follows 2,000 draws of a name: 2.5 to 3.4 s of the hair) and cannot read b4 or b7 (kept uploads, b7 bound outside 63CB0): its rows before the fade show the hair as `SAT_HairIbl2_Fwd_[A]` unblended (0x1F), `_DptA`, `_FwdSdw_[A]` blended (mesh 12) and `_Velo_[A]`. Next, first: what the preparation-2 row draws and whether it makes the dissolve; then its caller (the witness's return address, or GhidraMCP on 5C0E0's DATA references; shader pass 2 is the depth programme: whether its dithered depth removes the hair's colour through the colour row's depth test is INFERRED), then its transcription for the fading parts whose emit descriptor has byte +0x10 (CFDE70's pre-pass byte, `fade_prepass` natively; the hair).

Tranche 18's « none of 57A20's records is (0x29, 2) » read only the constant passes: in 57A20's branch for a view whose pass id (view +0x124) is 1..0x38 (140058dfa: `LEA EAX,[RBX-1]; CMP EAX,0x37; JA`; the cascades 1..4 and the local faces 9..0x38, I-0106; 5..8 not named here) and a category other than 0x27, 5C060 records one row (R8D = R14D, the model's category after the fade's remap of 57A20's start, which makes the fading hair 0x29; [RSP+0x28] = EDI = 2, set at 140058d9a; [RSP+0x30] = the class mask 0x107), once more for a second owner model (140058e5c) and once for the model (140058e94): the casters' row the native transcribes as `engine_pass==1` (10_options_and_passes.inc, preparation 2, shader pass 2 Dpt/DptA). So the row `reference_row_fade_inputs` saw alone at (0x29, 2) in `i113-t18-warp6-original` is the hair drawn into the sun's cascades (and local faces), and it alone reads +0x89 = 1: 5C0E0:101-108 takes +0x89 for one row of preparation 2, +0x88 otherwise. The original's fading hair therefore dithers its shadow from the fade's first step while its colour and pre-pass rows fade smoothly (+0x88 = 0, as the native's): the sparse pixels and the light silhouette of Thomas's clip are the head and hair losing the hair's shadow in a dither (INFERRED: the only dithered row of the part; not witnessed by a run that removes it). Natively the casters' row takes its dither from +0x88 (`input.pixel`'s fade term, 10_options_and_passes.inc:309, and PS b7's bit 20, 13b_prepare_model_frame.inc:797), so the hair keeps its full shadow, the « solid dark cap » of `warp3`. The raster transcription (the casters' row reading `alternate_fade_enabled`) is not made: the cascades and the local faces are raster shadows the traced shadows replace (D-0237, D-0250). With the rays the hair's slices cast (RtSliceCasts: a transparent slice) at full opacity until the part is gone (`RtOccluded` has no fade term; not witnessed live): passed to R, the rule to carry into the traced shadow: a part whose group has +0x89 set and its opacity (+0x124) under the category scalar casts with that opacity (INFERRED: the Dpt/DptA programme's dither coverage not read; by `_[A]`'s discard, about that fraction of the caster's texels is kept). The witness (the step review's M1): a native run through the warp with the casters' row taking +0x89 (`fade-shadow-alt.flag`, a diagnostic: `NativeFadeDither`, 01_state.inc, in the casters' row's pixel policy and PS b7's bit 20; `Run-I113Warp.ps1 -Runs native-alt`), `i113-t19-warp-native-alt` (20:14-20:19, build-v1): **the hair still fades as one smooth translucent cap** (`evidence/I-0113/t19/warp-alt-head3x.png`, 3x at 7.5 frames/s, against tranche 18's `warp3-head3x-native-top-original-bottom.png`; OBSERVED). So the shadow hypothesis is not supported, the diagnostic's effect not witnessed: whether the cascades drew the hair dithered during the fade is not shown (the named draw witness logged one row of pass 1, at ditherFadeAlpha 1, before the fade); a next witness journals a casters' row with `alternate_fade_enabled` and FC_DitherEnable during the fade. Read again, the original's frames show the background through sparse dark pixels of the hair, a screen-door on its colour (INFERRED from the pictures), which tranche 18's static chain (the colour row's FC_DitherEnable from +0x88 = 0) does not explain: gap 21 stays open; next, the original's own draws of the hair during the fade (the named oracle with a late window, `-SecondTravelNamedAfter`, on the colour and pre-pass rows: blend, depth, programme). The rule passed to R stays the original's (its casters dither) but is not the visible defect. `fade-shadow-alt.flag`, `NativeFadeDither` and `native-alt` stay as a named diagnostic until gap 21 closes, then go (the final review's L3). Tranche 20, the YEBIS tranche (Thomas, 2026-11-06 18:46, on E's recommendation: STUDY-YEBIS-REFUSALS' tone-map refusals; branch `claude/agitated-elgamal-2bdec8`; static only (GhidraMCP on the GUI's copy, the `ghidra-research` read re-checked by the pilot on every address below), the build `evidence/I-0113/build-w1` (its unit tests passed), no live run):
- **The value scale (ColorV) and the contrast (Contrust) served (OBSERVED in the code).** E69A0 builds the tone map's colour matrix in this order (1400e708d..1400e7117): reset (FUN_1416CBAE0), the hue rotation ([param_3+0xC4] ColorH), the saturation (+0xC8 ColorS), the value scale (+0xCC ColorV: FUN_1416CBC30, diag(v, v, v, 1)), then a fourth factor the native did not know: [+0x248] through FUN_1416CBC90 -> 1416FC150 -> FUN_1416D20C0(c, c, c), diag(c, c, c, 1) with row 3 ((1 - c) x [143D7EDB4] = 0.5) x3, a contrast about mid-grey; each post-multiplied by FUN_1416DD9B0, each only when its field differs from its identity value. param_3 is the YEBIS globals block 144961F70, which FUN_1400E42A0 fills from the blended Tone Map record: dword 7 ColorV at 14496203C (+0xCC), dword 8 **Contrust** at 1449621B8 (+0x248). Contrust is 1 in every world record and differs only in ten cutscene s-files (0.92 to 1.1625, `evidence/I-0066/queries/gparam-all`), the same files as most ColorV refusals: serving ColorV alone would have drawn those records without their contrast. `BuildTonemap` now applies ColorV and Contrust (word 8) in that order; `yebis_tonemap_test` checks the structure (every colour word times v, then times c, the offsets (1 - c)/2 in words 843, 847, 851) and that the witnessed zones' words are unchanged (m37, m31, m50, m45 bit for bit). Not witnessed against an original capture: no retail world record has ColorV or Contrust off 1 (`m36_00_0001`'s ColorV 1.2 is not retail and under auto exposure), and the Reference capture of the tone-map globals (`yebis-tonemap-capture.flag`) is no longer in the module.
- **The field-of-view vignette served (OBSERVED).** FUN_141751A60's power path computes v = (tan(pi/8) (1 - d) + d [glare+0x138]) power, d = VignetteFovDependence; FUN_141753790, called every frame by 1416E9A80 with the library's S+0x570, stores +0x138 = tan(fovy / 2) of E69A0's camera record +0x50 (W10 LS-2: the shafts read the same word), or tan([142A66FD0] = pi/8) when it is not positive. Natively `VignetteRect` takes d and tan(vertical fov / 2) of the frame's camera, with the same fallback (`VignetteFovTerm`); d = 0 gives the captured m31 rectangle bit for bit (the hardware tone-map test T12 passes); the tangent is the CPU's tanf, bit-identical to the captured +0x138 at the shaft captures' gameplay fovs (`yebis_shafts_test`); the shafts and the vignette now take the same `VignetteFovTerm` (the final review's L4); a NaN tangent stays NaN in FUN_141753790 (COMISS, JC) and natively refuses a vignette whose d != 0. Its scope is the cutscenes: 58 refused s-file records of 13 files (STUDY-YEBIS-REFUSALS §4).
- **Exposure 0 and gamma 0 served as the original (OBSERVED: the setter's guards and the draw's gamma; INFERRED: word 616, below).** The library's tone setter 1416F71B0 (E69A0's FUN_1416CA450) stores max(exposure, [143D7ECDC] = FLT_MIN) (COMISS, CMOVBE: zero, negative or NaN give FLT_MIN) and max(gamma, 0) (NaN 0), and FUN_141753F00 takes a zero gamma as [143D7EE90] = 2.2. So `m41_00_0900` (the Kiln of the First Flame's slot 9, Exposure 0) and the s-file records with 0 draw a black scene term rather than a skipped tone map (the sRGB composition the native showed instead); DLSS's exposure input takes 1 then, not FLT_MIN. Whether exposure FLT_MIN reaches word 616 unchanged (FUN_1416E5BC0 divides by [ctx+0x698] = 1, or by [ctx+0x69C] x exposure when [ctx+0x454] is set, a CPostEffect::Initialize flag not traced) is INFERRED from the witnessed zones, where word 616 is the record's exposure.

The named oracle now reads the device context's own bindings at the original's draw (`live`: b2, b4, b7, t38, s8; `i113-t22-warp3-original`): t38 is a 4x4 texture of format 65, A8_UNORM, one mip, at all 1648 named rows, those with FC_DitherEnable 0 included (OBSERVED; every row on a deferred context, so b2, b4 and b7 come from the bound buffers' last kept uploads and t38's texels were not read back: they are the code's), where the native bound 7ECB0's 64x64 R8G8B8A8 rotation noise (the SSAO's bank), whose alpha is 0 (`native_named_draw_sources`: `engine-common-dither`, 64, format 28): natively d = 0 on every pixel, so a dithered row discarded all its pixels as soon as its fade began. Seen so: forcing the dither on every fading row (a diagnostic, `i113-t22-warp-native-dither`) made the whole character vanish in one frame (`warp-head-forced-dither-before-fix.png`). The texture is FF630's (`[[GXRS]+0x4DE8]+0x8D0`, created 4x4 A8_UNORM by 14002AF40 from the 16 ints 0, 8, 2, 10, 12, 4, 14, 6, 3, 11, 1, 9, 15, 7, 13, 5 at 140100530, times 255 / 15; its pair at +0x940 is 255 minus each byte), bound at t38 by 13B450 as min(Q+0x1A6, 1) (VERIFIED, GhidraMCP; the `ghidra-research` read re-checked on 13B450's disassembly and 140100530's bytes). Natively t38 is now that pair (`ProduceModelFadeDitherTextures`; `fade_dither_test`: the texels, a fade of k / 16 keeping k pixels of a cell, a crossfade's two rows covering each pixel once); the second texture goes to a row whose fade intent is a LOD crossfade's second LOD (D06E10 draws it with its branch argument 1; that this is the descriptor byte +0x0B which 5C0E0 copies to Q+0x1A6 is INFERRED). With the fix, `i113-t22-warp5-native` (default rules): the hair breaks into sparse pixels while the body stays opaque, as the original's (`warp-head-fixed-native-top-original-bottom.png`, by eye on stills). In the original the fading hair's four meshes draw `Fwd_[A]` unblended with FC_DitherEnable 1 (77 of their `Fwd_[A]` rows with mulCol.w under 1; b7 read from its last kept upload, INFERRED the draw's), where the native blends them; but 101 of the 106 named rows of any programme (hair and face) with mulCol.w under 1 and FC_DitherEnable 1 read ditherFadeAlpha (b4 +0x100) 1, at which the fade test discards nothing: the kept b4 bytes are not the draw's, or +0x100 is not the instance drawn (open). The native's picture now looks like the original's without the unblended rule, which stays to read with the original's fade value at the draw. Wider (the terms VERIFIED in the disassembly and I-0117's census, their effect INFERRED, not witnessed): every dithered fade natively (a LOD crossfade, an object fading in or out with +0x88) discarded all its pixels before, so these fades popped; and the `_[A]` programmes' alpha test, a + (d - 0.5) x FC_AlphaRef.y < FC_AlphaRef.x, reads the same d whatever FC_DitherEnable, so the materials with FC_AlphaRef.y not 0 (I-0068's `all-mtd.tsv`: `C[ARSN]_di` and `_di_Cloth` (255, 255), `Eclipse`, `M[Eclipse]`, `C[Eclipse]` (80, 255), `P[ChrCustom_Skin]_SSS_Dither` (100, 255), `S[A]_add` (5, 5)) change too: a `_di` part was cut on every pixel natively (invisible), it now keeps the pattern's share, and the Eclipse and SSS_Dither edges are dithered, as the original binds them. Tranche 23 (branch `claude/cranky-wu-bcf0b0`, 2026-11-07 07:55 to 11:00, the ASUS; builds `evidence/I-0113/build-y1` (the witnesses, then the A/B), `-y2`, `-y3` (the fix as run at the bridge, main with R7c merged in), `-y5` (the tree that lands: the final review's fixes, main merged in), every unit test passed in each; three announced slots, 08:33-08:47, 09:26-09:40, 10:30-10:36, every run offline, restored by hash, no fault):
- **Gap 22 (the Irithyll barrier): its cause found and fixed (OBSERVED in the films, VERIFIED in the decompilation).** PS 2AC7 (`GXFfxdistortionBump`, `fxc /dumpbin` of the dumped `ffx-ps-2AC7.dxbc`) is the heat-haze distortion: it writes the frame copy t0 sampled at an offset (a scrolled normal map, at most 0.01 x g_waveStrength), unblended (bank +0x08 in both modes, FFX-DRAW 2026-10-17). The witness `ffx_distortion_series` (new, `ffx-series.flag`, both modes, slot 27 read after its call: the draw's PS image and its VS image's g_worldViewProjectionMatrix) shows natively (`i113-t23-dist-native`) the barrier's quads in cross-fading pairs (g_color.w and g_waveStrength rising for one while falling for the other, about 60 frames each), overlapping, drawn back to front; with one copy before the pass only the nearest quad's distortion shows, and each birth or death on top resets it: the far scene jumps, its mist « starts again from another position ». The original (`i113-t23-dist2-original`, plain Reference with `ffx-draw-observe`; the original-probe setup returns before the effect witnesses, so `i113-t23-dist-original` wrote none: Run-Endurance now runs plain Reference when `ffx-draw-observe` is in its flags) calls, before each of its distortion quads, a `CopySubresourceRegion` (`ffx_distortion_series_copy`: 1,031 region copies in its 180 frames, as many as quads, on the drawing thread, into the R16G16B16A16 frame copy from the target): each quad distorts the ones drawn before it. It also makes two full copies of the target into the same frame copy every frame (reconciled in tranche 29: the first 103510's key 0x29 copy, the second recorded by the effect side after the quads, « Tranche 29 »), one recorded on another thread about 0.03 ms before the frame's first region copy, one on the drawing thread after the quads (their GPU order against pass 0x2D not read; the final review's M2: FFX-FAMILIES-PHASE2 §8 knew of one). The copy is the draw context's slot 140260440 (GhidraMCP): the quad's screen rectangle (normalized) times the bound target's size ([dc+0x20]+0x4F8), floored and ceiled, 16 pixels wider on each side, clamped to the target, copied from the target into the frame copy ([dc+0xA70]+8) at the same place through 1920280 when the two textures' formats are equal; a view array at [dc+0xA88] with more than one view gives the box (0, 0) to [+0x7C] + 2 x [+0x78], [+0x80] + 2 x [+0x78]. Natively the target was never a GX texture, so the slot copied nothing, without a trap or a row. The study thread S found the same copy in the draw context's slot 20 (262A70, the trails' appearance setup, 606 and 10012), over the whole target when the FXR property at the resource's +0x280 is above 0 (stored at dc+0x5A0), and 140260440's caller 1403A02C0 (608's radial blur, STUDY-SILENT-CALLS).
- **The fix** (CopyRect): effect_draw hooks 140260440 (ours when [[dc+0x100]+8] is the native inner context; nothing without a frame copy) and 262A70 (after the original); each emits a CopyRect statement in the stream (the rectangle, the whole target or the +0xA88 box); the effect stage hands it to the scene pass outside the half-size layer, which copies that box from the lit HDR into the scene copy at the same place (a `copy_rect` of its own, `CopyTextureRegion`), its two barriers local to it (the HDR and the copy back in the states the effect passes declare), so no graph pass and nothing in the plan key whatever the number of quads (the final review's M3; the step review's key words and early-stop declarations, written first, removed with it). The full effect copy before the first pass that samples the copy (Native2027) stays beside the per-quad copies, as the original's full copies (M2; it also keeps the layered 0x31 draws' copy, M1). The A/B flag `ffx-copy-each` (a full copy before each statement of the pass, this tranche's) was the discriminating run: `i113-t23-each-native` none of the jumps; it is removed.
- **Seen** (`scripts/video/videosheet.py`, the far region 820:80:440:150, the floor as control, 40 s films of Gamma's visit): `i113-t23-dist-native` (the native before) 5 jumps (frames 157, 278, 400, 461, 523); `-each-native` (the A/B) 0; `-dist2-original` 0; `-fix-native` (the fix as first written, build-y3, its copies graph passes and without the full copy before 0x2D) 0, 46,822 CopyRect statements handed to the copy (`native_effect_stage` `copy_rects`; an empty box among them copies nothing), no effect failure (OBSERVED); the landing tree's form (local barriers, the full copy kept) not run live (D-0229: the start check after the merge). Not seen: the Firelink bonfire's fire (the same pass and programmes, INFERRED fixed too); the trails' copy (262A70) and 608's radial blur in play.
- Also: the `native_ffx_trap` rows are already written by default (the 1st, 100th and 10,000th call of a slot); none in the 60 latest native runs; a call that does nothing natively without reaching a trap (as 140260440) is the study thread's audit (STUDY-SILENT-CALLS, Thomas 2026-11-07). From its row 3: each new kind of skipped effect command (a renderer class outside `allowed[]`, an entity that is not a node draw item) now writes one `native_ffx_skipped` row by default (at most 64 a session); its row 2 is the trails' hook above. Every native run writes about 7,200 `graph_barrier_mismatch` rows (`env_regions`, `rr_specular_hit_distance`, expected 4, actual 192), already before this tranche (`i113-t23-each-native`, `start-f03c4dfb`), and with them the frame graph's executor is nearly off: in `i113-t23-fix-native` 13,430 of 13,562 frames mismatch and 130 ran a cached plan (`graph_shadow`); `start-f03c4dfb`, `start-13d1c9c5` and `start-24691870` ran none where `start-c56ce740` ran 676 of 687 (the final review's M4): told to R through the coordinator. - **Gap 23: the hair's colour programme cannot cut the fade with the constants the original binds (READ in its disassembly, `t22/SAT_HairIbl2_FwdSdw_A.asm` lines 995-1009).** It discards a pixel when (FC_DitherEnable > 0 and FC_isDitherAlpha and (d − 0.5) × 0.999 + ditherFadeAlpha < 0.5), when FC_AlphaRef.x > 0 and the output alpha (texture alpha × mulCol.w) is exactly 0, or when (d − 0.5) × FC_AlphaRef.y + a² < FC_AlphaRef.x, a² the squared texture alpha without the fade (line 452) and d the 4x4 t38 at the pixel / 4. At the original's draws (`i113-t34-warp-original`, the oracle's « ctx » rows of the fade, 4.0 to 5.3 s) FC_DitherEnable is 0 on every colour row (blended 5/6), FC_AlphaRef is (0.502, 0, …) or (1/255, 0, 1, 0), so .y = 0, and alpha to coverage is off (`blend.a2c` 0 on every row): no term follows the fade. The pre-pass rows of the same programme family (cull back, no slope) are drawn at every fade step with FC_DitherEnable 0 too; only the casters (cull front or slope 3) take 1. So either the oracle's constants are not what the GPU read, or the pattern is made elsewhere (another draw, another state, or the picture's anti-aliasing: the original has no temporal AA and the native's DLAA may smooth a thin strand's aliasing into a translucent cap, INFERRED, tested below).   three others' not) the camera did not move (`evidence/I-0113/t36/g26-holds-nofix.jpg`, `g26-holds-fix.jpg`, a shot every 5 s). Tranche 41 (branch `claude/inspiring-tu-224c01` from `main` `8062341a`, 2026-11-12 from 02:00, a fresh E thread; budget 130 of 140):
tranche 40's « Handed on », in order.
- **Why the reactive weighting did not keep the fade (READ in `taa_shader.h`; the cause INFERRED, tested below).** The weight was taken
  per pixel, where the 0x29 rows or the effects changed it against the base. A pixel they leave as it was (a hole of the dissolving
  hair, the place a moving fading arm has just left) kept the full history weight, and the neighbourhood's variance box, which spans
  the body's and the background's colours beside a drawn pixel, does not clip that history: the body's past frames stay there (the
  soft cap, the second outline). The change (`13992c76`): the difference is the largest over the Resolve's 3x3 (FSR 2 dilates its
  reactive mask the same way, connaissance générale), read from the base's nine texels; `taa-reactive-view.flag` (a test view,
  `Run-StartupTrace`'s flag list) paints the weight taken red in the output (not in the history, not with the sharpening).
  `taa_test` T13: an opaque square in the history, then the square dissolved to every other pixel over a black base: a hole beside
  drawn dots keeps the square's history without the base (0.87 by hand) and takes its current colour with it (0.21; the per-pixel
  form gives 0.87 there too, by hand); the view red in the output only (the next frame unchanged). `build-t41a` (02:06-02:18, before T13, every
  unit test passed) is the one the slot runs. **Step review** (a fresh Opus at high effort, `1b48e56d..9222588d`): nothing High or
  Medium, criteria 1 to 5 met, T13's figures checked by hand; L1 a shadowed name in the shader (renamed), L2 the view silent when it
  cannot paint (then `reactive_view` in the `native_taa_frames` row, since replaced by `view_frames`: the final review's L1).
  **A Fable 5.2 read** (Thomas, 03:40: « Pour les fils qui n'ont rien à faire, ils peuvent lancer un sous agent Fable 5.2 qui vérifie
  leur code »; read-only during the full control, `1b48e56d..34a4a886`): no High and no bug in the code. M1 the 3x3 clears a vacated
  pixel only within one pixel of a change: a body moving d pixels a frame leaves d - 1 pixels at the full history weight (its proposal:
  the weight taken kept in the history's free alpha, so a pixel once changed stays reactive; not done before the films). M2 the « two
  outlines » are in the original too (« Tranche 33 »: both films show the arm's and the back's overlapping outlines, the 0x29 body
  blended with no pre-pass): the step rests on the hair's holes, the `no-taa` control first. M3 (a forecast) the hair may come out as
  jittered, shimmering dots rather than the original's steady ones: judged on film, not on the Sobel alone. L1 the dilation's ring
  around effects and 0x29 rows (static rows not witnessed, as before); L2 T13 did not bound the radius (now: one changed pixel takes
  the weight one pixel away, not two); L3 the view's red enters the post chain (a test view); L4 the row's `reactive_view` every 3,600
  frames (kept); L5 eight more base reads a pixel (unmeasured).

## Uncertainty / unresolved questions

- **Gap 27 (Thomas, 2026-11-11 08:15; fixed in tranche 36 on the terrain field, Thomas's play the judge, its record there; the history of the report below): locomotion defects on
  stairs with a rate above 60 frames/s set and the game below 60.** His play sessions at 144 frames/s (`%LOCALAPPDATA%/DS3-DX12/play-20261111-073907.jsonl`, `play-20261111-080849.jsonl`,
  `build-r12g20`) had every unlock correction active (the `unlock_fix` row: locomotion, terrain, the follow camera's laws and the
  rest); his next session runs at DS3's 60 (the page's frame rate set back). Known: I-0114 corrected the terrain response and found
  the terrain field's level equal at 60 and 240 on one staircase (eighth tranche), its open point the line the 240 walk took; TRTF's
  decay branch never validated (I-0112 §3). The place, the staircase and what he saw (speed, sliding, a stutter) to ask; then the
  A/B at 60 and 144 on that staircase with `no-terrain-fix.flag`.
  Thomas (08:16): the defect with the setting at 144, none with it at 60, the game running at about 20 frames/s in both (PT, four
  bounces, NVIDIA DLAA): it follows the setting, not the real rate. At 144 the module writes the requested rate and its enable
  byte (clock +0x354, +0x358) and the corrections take k = dt·60, about 3 at 20 frames/s; at 60 neither. INFERRED leads: the
  corrections were validated only above 60 (k < 1: 165 to 240 frames/s, I-0114), never at k > 1; or the game's own unlocked mode
  (the enable byte) treats a long frame unlike its capped mode. The A/B at about 20 frames/s on a staircase: the setting 144 with
  and without `no-unlock-fix.flag`, against the setting 60. A candidate if the first lead holds: the laws applied only for k < 1.
  Thomas's clips (08:17, 08:19, `C:/Users/User/Videos/NVIDIA/Dark Souls III/`, « descente escalier original E27 » 9.6 s 3200x1440
  and « descente d'escalier + remontée natif E27 » 16.8 s 3840x2160): the same staircase down; at two pictures a second the descent
  and its timing look alike at that sampling. Thomas (08:25, 08:28, two stills at about 4 s and 5 s): the character hops and stands
  far too high on the steps (the hat's tip at the candle altar's top natively, at the floor behind in the original): the terrain
  field's level, the quantity I-0114's eighth tranche found equal at 60 and 240 (k < 1), here at k about 3. Thomas allows a Fable 5.2
  RCA if it resists (08:30, not urgent, mostly for E).
